# Proposed OEIS comments: A197396 to A203060

One section per OEIS entry: the date the result was obtained, the paper that proves it, what the entry still records as unsettled, and the text proposed for posting on that entry.

**Nothing here has been posted.** See [../SUBMITTING.md](../SUBMITTING.md).

---

## A197396

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A197396>  
Result obtained: **31 August 2026**  
Paper: [9051](../papers/09001-09500/09051-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = a(n-1) +2*a(n-2) +8*a(n-3) +50*a(n-4) +65*a(n-5) +125*a(n-6) -4*a(n-7) -330*a(n-8) -596*a(n-9) -2336*a(n-10) -1872*a(n-11) +712*a(n-12) +520*a(n-13) +4176*a(n-14) +4400*a(n-15) -1728*a(n-16) -384*a(n-17)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 15 625 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 18. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

Paper: [10476](../papers/10001-10500/10476-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 2*a(n-1) +2*a(n-2) +19*a(n-3) +10*a(n-4) +21*a(n-5) +31*a(n-6) -99*a(n-7) -9*a(n-8) +16*a(n-9).

Proposed OEIS comment:

```

```

## A200250

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A200250>  
Result obtained: **13 September 2026**  
Paper: [4502](../papers/04501-05000/04502-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = a(n-1) +9*a(n-2) +24*a(n-3) +36*a(n-4) +35*a(n-5) +21*a(n-6) +7*a(n-7) +a(n-8).
> Empirical g.f.: x*(2 + x)*(2 + 2*x + x^2)*(2 + 4*x + 6*x^2 + 4*x^3 + x^4) / (1 - x - 9*x^2 - 24*x^3 - 36*x^4 - 35*x^5 - 21*x^6 - 7*x^7 - x^8). - _Colin Barker_, May 20 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical column recurrences are true. Each column counts arrays of a fixed width, and counting those a row at a time gives a transfer matrix, so every column satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most the number of states; checking the ones above is then a finite exact computation, and they hold. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. ```

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
This follows from the recurrence already stated on the entry, which is not labelled a conjecture: in the Ore algebra Q(n)[N], N the shift, the conjectured operator is a left multiple of that one, so it annihilates the sequence as well. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 25 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 0. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A202048

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A202048>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [4590](../papers/04501-05000/04590-PROOF.pdf), [2499](../papers/02001-02500/02499-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = (1/30)*n^6 + (9/10)*n^5 + (59/6)*n^4 + (111/2)*n^3 + (2552/15)*n^2 + (1278/5)*n + 144.
> Conjectures from _Colin Barker_, May 25 2018: (Start)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 157 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. ```

## A202440

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A202440>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [5385](../papers/05001-05500/05385-PROOF.pdf), [2503](../papers/02501-03000/02503-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = (2/3)*n^3 + 8*n^2 + (100/3)*n + 34 for n>1.
> Conjectures from _Colin Barker_, May 28 2018: (Start)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 43 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A202464

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A202464>  
Result obtained: **7 September 2026**  
Paper: [11587](../papers/11501-12000/11587-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 4*a(n-1) +8*a(n-2) +18*a(n-3) -6*a(n-4) +2*a(n-5) -a(n-6).
> Empirical g.f.: x*(265 + 513*x + 841*x^2 - 281*x^3 + 91*x^4 - 49*x^5) / (1 - 4*x - 8*x^2 - 18*x^3 + 6*x^4 - 2*x^5 + x^6). - _Colin Barker_, Jun 01 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. The condition is carried by a bounded amount of state down the array -- a window of three consecutive lines where it names only cells one step apart, and otherwise a row sum together with one flag per adjacent column pair -- so counting the arrays a line at a time gives a transfer matrix with 7 states, and a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 4. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

> Empirical: a(n) = 28*a(n-1) -119*a(n-2) +5320*a(n-3) +14657*a(n-4) +262356*a(n-5) +711785*a(n-6) +4372840*a(n-7) +8450903*a(n-8) +26917092*a(n-9) +32103217*a(n-10) +55870264*a(n-11) +31144464*a(n-12) +25401600*a(n-13)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 2 401 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 14. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```
